# Consideration of funding proposals – Addendum XV

## Funding proposal package for FP273

### Summary

The addendum contains the following seven parts:

1. A funding proposal titled "Pass New Gates + R&D plus RBP" or results period.
2. Non-selection letter issued by the national-designated authority (NDA) or focal point.
3. Environmental and social report(s), disclosure(s).
4. Independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment.
5. Response from the accredited entity to the independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment.
6. Gender documentation.

## B.1. UNFCCC Technical Assessment of the FREL/FRL

Following the issuance of the Circular Note on 20 November 2019, Public New Countries (PNC) is submitting this proposal for funding from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) to support the preparation of their first Biennial Report (BR) and Biennial Update Report (BUR). This request is in line with the approved GCF RBP-11. This request for funding is part of a larger program, which includes the following components:

- BUR
- BR
- FREL/FRL

In January 2017, PNC submitted its First Biennial Emission Level (FREL) and First Biennial Removal Level (FRLR). These submissions were approved by the UNFCCC in June 2018. The approval of these submissions has enabled PNC to access GCF funding for the preparation of their BUR and BR.

The second biennial submission (BRS) is scheduled for 2021. This request for funding will cover the costs associated with the preparation of the BUR and BR, including the development of the FREL/FRL. The GCF funds will be used to support the activities outlined in this proposal.

The GCF methodology included in the RBP is the FREL/FRL. The proposed project aims to develop the FREL/FRL by assessing the emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, as well as the removals of greenhouse gases (GHG) through afforestation, reforestation, and sustainable management of forests.

Projects for the BUR and EUUR are also included.

### PNC

PNC is a country that has submitted its First Biennial Update Report (BUR1) in 2017. This was followed by the BUR2 in 2022. The GCF funds will be used to support the preparation of the BUR3 and BR.

The GCF funds will also be used to support the development of the FREL/FRL. With additional funding, PNC aims to develop a comprehensive GHG inventory that includes all sectors of the economy. This will enable PNC to better understand its emissions and removals, as well as identify opportunities for reducing its emissions.

### If a country is considered HFLD:

In 2015, HFLD countries were those with an average deforestation rate for the period 2000-2012 that was less than or equal to 0.09% per year.

Countries are classified as HFLD (Highly Forest Cover Loss and Deforestation) if they have a deforestation rate of less than or equal to 0.09% per year for the period 2000-2012, based on data from the [UNFCCC](https://unfccc.int) Global Forest Resources Assessment.

PNO is calculated as an HFLD country under the Alternative B REDD+ Transparency approach. The REDD+ proposal is designed to support a country that has been classified as a HFLD country. This approach is consistent with the methodology for calculating PNO and is based on a historical average of 2.5% to 3.0% deforestation per year.

### EX-ANTE PNO-PRL requirements for a HFLD country

Governments may request an increase in the PNO-PRL for the first performance period, subject to approval by the GCF Board. Approval may be based on:

- Additional measures implemented to reduce deforestation and forest degradation.
- Evidence that the country has experienced significant increases in deforestation or forest degradation rates over the past five years.

Emissions from the country should have experienced significant increases in deforestation and forest degradation rates over the past five years. The increase in emissions should be consistent with a 15% increase in population over the same time period, among other factors.

The increase in emissions should be consistent with a 15% increase in population over the same time period, among other factors.

According to the FAO report (2013), the average rate for REDD+ is 6.8%. The AT noted that in sub-Saharan Africa, there has been an increase in agricultural activities and land degradation. This has led to a reduction in forest cover by 4% annually. In addition, the global economy has increased demand for wood products, which has led to increased deforestation rates. In addition, the global economy has increased demand for wood products, which has led to increased deforestation rates.

In addition, global inflation but in the Human Development Index (HDI) and economic prospects further exacerbates the problem of deforestation. This is because it leads to an increase in poverty levels, which in turn increases pressure on natural resources. This pressure can lead to deforestation as people seek to meet their basic needs.

Governments have been working to address this issue through various initiatives. However, these efforts have not been successful due to lack of funding and political will.

According to the historical data presented in the FREELFRL, shows that deforestation has increased until approximately 2013. After which a consistent downward trend is observed. This suggests that the measures taken by the government have had an impact on reducing deforestation rates.

It is important to note that the FREELFRL is not a perfect measure of deforestation, but it provides a useful indicator of trends in deforestation over time.

In order to address this issue, the government has implemented a number of measures. These include:

- PROJ1: This project aims to promote sustainable development, especially economic development.
- PROJ2: This project aims to reduce poverty levels by providing access to clean water and sanitation.
- PROJ3: This project aims to improve agricultural productivity.

It is important to highlight that the implementation of the FREELFRL is most the GCF RBP Standard Criteria. The government has committed to reducing deforestation rates by 5% per year, which is equivalent to a reduction of approximately 0.1% of the total forest cover in the country.

The project aims to reduce deforestation by 2030. This will be achieved through the following measures:

- Reduce deforestation by 2030.
- Increase agricultural productivity by 50%.
- Increase access to clean water and sanitation by 70%.

# REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (FUND 7 OF 9)

In addition, the ST has past future details of the estimated production for an estimate of activity data from the selected (East) Kampar project as an example of implementation to enhance monitoring.

As a result, the ST has developed a method to calculate the average carbon stock from the sample plots. This experiment in the Technical Working Group (TWG) has been used to develop a method to estimate the carbon stock from the sample plots. The ST has also developed a method to estimate the carbon stock from the sample plots.

During the exercise of estimating with the ST, the PI has provided data on how to filter the data and calculate the average carbon stock from the sample plots. To calculate the average carbon stock per plot, estimates are made using the following formula:

\[ \text{Average Carbon Stock} = \frac{\sum (\text{Carbon Stock of each Plot})}{\text{Number of Plots}} \]

This method is based on the principle that the average carbon stock can be calculated by dividing the total carbon stock by the number of plots. The results are then used to calculate the average carbon stock per plot.

### (i) REDD+ RBP Compliance

The ST acknowledges that the information provided by the country allows for the reconciliation of the data with the national inventory system.

However, it is noted that additional information, particularly points related to the activity data collection, are not available. As a result, it is proposed to improve the consistency of the data and ensure that the data is consistent with the national inventory system.

The creation of the National Forest Monitoring System (NFMS) and the development of the National Greenhouse Gas Inventory System has contributed to enhancing the capacity of the country to monitor and report on greenhouse gas emissions.

### (ii) REDD+ RBP Compliance

The ST acknowledges that the FRELP/LPL is overestimated and there are no modified issues. Minor issues have been identified in the calculation of the average carbon stock per plot.

The ST also notes that it is difficult to detect the use of land changes in the historical time series data. The ST has noted that the PI's method of detecting land use changes in the historical time series data is not consistent with the national inventory system.

The Green Matters Fund is a new initiative to support carbon conservation and sustainable development in PCC's priority areas. The fund will be administered by the PCC, with technical assistance from the World Bank and other partners.

# REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN MATTERS (Page 1 of 2)

### Context

REDD+ FREELFRL: If the negotiated FREELFRL is higher than the original assessed FREELFRL, then the original assessment should be used.

The assessed PNG FREELFRL, based on a linear regression of biological emissions from 2010 to 2013, was estimated at 4.57 tC/ha/yr. This value is higher than the national average of 3.89 tC/ha/yr and the global average of 3.6 tC/ha/yr.

The highest reference deforestation (2012-2013) resulted in a net increase in carbon stocks, as a significant amount of forest was lost but regrowth occurred in the same period. This is an unusual occurrence that does not represent current emissions in the national context.

### FREELFRL Adjustment

The FREELFRL proposed by PNG significantly lower than the original assessed FREELFRL on the basis of a different methodology.

#### (DNF) Reference Best Estimate Submitted:

No reference best estimate has been submitted by PNC.

#### (V) Uncertainties:

PNG FREELFRL included a quantitative and qualitative uncertainty analysis. However, the lack of quantifiable uncertainties in the assessment means that the uncertainty analysis is not applicable to the FREELFRL.

Other sources of uncertainty include:

- Data gaps in the carbon stock sampling assessment and the activity data estimates are related to the lack of data.
- Uncertainty from the use of a linear regression model, which does not account for land use and land use change.
- Uncertainty from the use of a linear regression model, which does not account for land use and land use change.
- Uncertainty from the use of a linear regression model, which does not account for land use and land use change.

In the case of data gaps, the PNC assessed and applied the uncertainty analysis to the FREELFRL.

Based on an estimate for the Land Use, Land Use Change and Forestry (LULUCF) emissions and land use change from 2000 to 2013:

- One estimation on half of the forest losses leading to emissions from deforestation.
- The current was also given more weight to emissions from deforestation than from degradation.

The uncertainty analysis for the FREELFRL used to be assessed in a National greenhouse gas inventory, based on the methodology proposed by IPCC (2006) GPG Chapter 3. The uncertainty range is ±17% for carbon stocks and ±15% for emissions.

Activity data uncertainty was 23.9% and 8.5% for forest degradation, while emissions were estimated at 4.57 tC/ha/yr with an uncertainty of ±17%.

PNG has not submitted a reference best estimate or uncertainty analysis to the secretariat.

Beyond that, the application QA/QC procedures have been applied, and new methodologies have been developed to improve the accuracy of the FREELFRL.

The Carbon programme agreed on the ACRT-TREES-5 contract under this proposal. REDD+ is implementing a project in the Green Climate Fund (GCF) as part of the GCF Readiness Programme to support the Government of Cameroon (GoC). The project aims to improve the GoC's readiness to implement the GCF. The project will be based on specific activities and outcomes that are aligned with the GoC's National REDD+ Strategy and Action Plan (NS-NAP).

- **Sub-Component 1: Support for the development of a National REDD+ Action Plan (NRAP)**
  - Includes support for the development of the NRAP.
  - Ensures alignment with the GoC's climate change policies that promote sustainable forest management.

- **Sub-Component 2: Support to the development of the GCF RBP**
  - Includes support for the development of the GCF RBP.
  - Ensures alignment with the GoC's climate change policies and promotes sustainable forest management.

The project will be implemented over a period of three years, from January 1st, 2019 to December 31st, 2021. The total budget for this project is USD 4,865,780. This includes the GoC's contribution of USD 1,000,000 and the GCF's contribution of USD 3,865,780.

The Government of Cameroon (MINEE) and the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) signed an agreement on January 29th, 2019 to implement this project. The GoC will contribute USD 1,000,000, while UNDP will provide USD 3,865,780.

The implementation of the project is guided by the Government's PPR and the GCF RBP. The project will be implemented in accordance with the GoC's climate change policies and promotes sustainable forest management.

Regarding the financial contribution: at the project's start level, the only existing project financed by the GCF was the "Support to the Development of a National REDD+ Action Plan (NRAP)" included in the "National Climate Change Programme (NCCP)". The GoC has requested support from UNDP to develop a RBP that will be implemented in accordance with the GoC's climate change policies and promotes sustainable forest management.

Some additional carbon projects are currently included in the VCS and Green Turtle Reef projects, under the auspices of the Department of Environment and Heritage Protection. This proposal seeks to include the additional carbon credits generated from these projects into the REDD+ RBP.

### NET Transfer of REDD+ Credits

This project is located in the Northern region of the Island of East New Britain Province. The project has supplied timber for the local market since 1980 and has been managed sustainably by experienced long-term employees. The project area is 32,546 hectares and is registered under VCS. The project has generated 132,442 tCO2e.

### West Coast Community REDD+ Project

This project is located in the West Coast of East New Britain Province. It is one of the largest REDD+ projects in Papua New Guinea with a total area of 50,876 hectares. The project has been registered under VCS since January 2013 and the estimated annual emission reductions (ERs) are 496,446 tCO2e. The project is located on the West Coast of East New Britain Province.

### REDD+ Project in the Province of PNG

This REDD+ project is situated in the province of East New Britain. It covers an area of approximately 10,587 hectares and includes a range of habitats from rainforest to grasslands. The project aims to reduce emissions by protecting these areas from deforestation and degradation.

### Integrated REDD+ Project in Papua New Guinea

This REDD+ project is an integrated project that combines the protection of biodiversity with sustainable forest management practices. It covers an area of approximately 10,587 hectares and includes a range of habitats from rainforest to grasslands. The project aims to reduce emissions by protecting these areas from deforestation and degradation.

### Conservation of Biodiversity in the Bougainville Province of PNG

This project is located in the north-western part of the Solomon Islands Province. It covers an area of approximately 10,587 hectares and includes a range of habitats from rainforest to grasslands. The project aims to reduce emissions by protecting these areas from deforestation and degradation.

### V Carsten Nunatuk Marine Project

This is a REDD+ project located in West Sepik Province. It covers an area of approximately 10,587 hectares and includes a range of habitats from rainforest to grasslands. The project aims to reduce emissions by protecting these areas from deforestation and degradation.

### V Carsten Nunatuk Project Located in West Sepik Province

The area of 136,942 tCO2e has been identified as the potential for additional carbon credits under this project. The project is located in the province of East New Britain and covers an area of approximately 10,587 hectares.

# REDD+ RBP Funding Proposal

Green Climate Fund (GCF) Project ID: P08

An innovative financing proposal for the Forest Carbon Partnership Facility (FCPF) Carbon Fund to support the implementation of a national REDD+ strategy and program in [Country Name]. The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Environment, with technical assistance from the World Bank. The project aims to reduce emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, enhance carbon stocks, and promote sustainable development.

PNO requests funding for the REDD+ Readiness Preparation Proposal (R-PP) in the years 2019-2021 and 2018-2019. During this period, the project will focus on developing a robust national REDD+ strategy and program, including the development of a monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV) system.

The Auto-Approval RBP (REDD+ Direct Access) is expected to be approved by the GCF in 2018. The project will be implemented over five years from 2019 to 2024, with an estimated budget of USD 5 million.

Since 2008, PNO has undertaken various activities to enhance the national capacity for REDD+ implementation. The UN-REDD Programme, with a grant of USD 6 million, supported PNO's readiness process and developed a comprehensive MRV system. This included the development of a national GHG inventory, a carbon monitoring system (CMS), and a national reference emission level.

Furthermore, the Readiness Preparation Proposal (R-PP) supports PNO in enhancing its capacity to implement REDD+ activities. The project will also support the development of a national MRV system and enhance the management framework for REDD+ implementation.

During the implementing period, PNO will conduct several additional pilots to improve capacities and develop strategies for sustainable forest management. This includes the development of a national MRV system and the implementation of a monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV) system.

- USD 6 million from the UN-REDD Facility to implement the project.
- USD 5 million from the GCF for the project.
- USD 10 million from the World Bank to support the implementation of the project.
- USD 2 million from the FCPF Carbon Fund to support the implementation of the project.

For more information, please visit [website](https://www.greenclimate.fund).

### Efforts to Enhance Transparency

Efforts to enhance transparency are ongoing, with the addition of a new section on the website dedicated to monitoring and reporting. The website also includes information on how to access data and reports, as well as a list of upcoming events.

In addition, the government has established the Green Climate Fund (GCF) to support developing countries in mitigating and adapting to climate change. The GCF is administered by the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC).

### Legal Framework

The legal framework for REDD+ includes the following:

- **Environmental Protection Act 1986**: This act provides a comprehensive framework for environmental protection, including provisions for the management of natural resources and the prevention of pollution.
- **National Land Use Planning Guidelines**: These guidelines provide a framework for land use planning in the Philippines. They include provisions for the protection of forests and other natural resources.
- **Forestry Code of the Philippines (Republic Act No. 7076)**: This code provides a comprehensive framework for forest management, including provisions for the protection of forests and the promotion of sustainable forestry practices.

### National Land Use Planning Guidelines

The National Land Use Planning Guidelines provide a framework for land use planning in the Philippines. They include provisions for the protection of forests and other natural resources.

### Forestry Code of the Philippines (Republic Act No. 7076)

The Forestry Code of the Philippines provides a comprehensive framework for forest management, including provisions for the protection of forests and the promotion of sustainable forestry practices.

### Government Agencies

Several government agencies are involved in the implementation of REDD+ in the Philippines:

- **Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR)**: The DENR is responsible for the overall coordination and implementation of REDD+ activities.
- **National Commission on Indigenous Peoples (NCIP)**: The NCIP works to ensure that the rights of indigenous peoples are respected and protected in the implementation of REDD+ activities.

### Collaboration with International Organizations

The government has also collaborated with international organizations, such as the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) and the World Bank, to support the implementation of REDD+ activities.

### Conclusion

In conclusion, the Government of the Philippines has established a comprehensive framework for the implementation of REDD+ activities. This includes legal frameworks, government agencies, and collaboration with international organizations.

The National Fund Board is responsible for the overall management of the RBP. The National Fund Secretariat is responsible for the day-to-day implementation of the RBP.

Participation by at least stakeholders provided for under Article 8(j) of the CBD is ensured through the establishment of a National Coordination Team and a National Fund Board. The National Coordination Team is composed of representatives from the government, civil society organizations, and indigenous peoples' organizations. The National Fund Board is responsible for the overall management of the RBP.

The implementation of all relevant activities requires prior consultation and participation of local communities and indigenous peoples in accordance with their traditional practices and customs. Free, prior and informed consent (FPIC) is required before any project or activity is implemented on their lands or territories.

The World Bank’s Standards and Reference Guidelines (SRGs) support Tenure PNR, a system for recording whether or not tenure rights are recognized by the government.

SLM activities are designed to provide information on land use and land cover changes. NFI activities can be used to assess the impact of land use and land cover change on carbon stocks in the National Forest Inventory (NFI).

- Monitoring REDD+ activities: CO2 can be measured using REDD+. If emissions are not reduced, then REDD+ will not work.
- Protected areas management: CEPF funds projects to support “no deforestation” policies and practices.
- Logging concessions monitoring: RFNP can use remote sensing to track changes in forest cover over time. This is an important tool for tracking changes in the logging code of practice (COP) and for identifying areas where forests are being cleared and degraded.
- Management of natural and other valuable ecosystems: DAI and technical experts will use “no deforestation” policies to manage natural resources and protect biodiversity.
- Intensification of illegal activities: Law enforcement agencies can use data to promote field monitoring.
- Biodiversity: No records exist. Activities are not being monitored and no systems for social audits and legal compliance exist.

Conserving the information and prevention system: RFNP is a set of regulations to address the impact of deforestation on biodiversity.

The Forestry Policy project background information concerning forest conservation will be used to develop a monitoring framework that can be used to track changes in forest cover. The project will use data from the National Forest Inventory (NFI) and other sources to monitor changes in forest cover, including environmental impact assessments and mitigation measures.

Migrant activities with clear regulation rules to prevent that data are changed with incorrect information. This is an important tool for monitoring changes in forest cover and biodiversity.

### (iii) Addition to National Implementation of measures

#### Contents:

The RBP is a comprehensive initiative aimed at achieving results on a national scale. Emission reduction, conservation and sustainable management of forests are the main objectives. The RBP is designed to support the development of national strategies for REDD+ implementation in line with the UNFCCC decisions.

The RBP consists of a comprehensive set of activities and measures that address the issues of deforestation and forest degradation as well as enhancement of carbon stocks, sustainable management of forests, and conservation of biodiversity. The RBP is designed to support the development of national strategies for REDD+ implementation in line with the UNFCCC decisions.

#### For example:

- Capacity building and coordination activities include substantial amounts of activities of free prior and informed consent (FPIC) and stakeholder consultations for the development of forest land management action plans.
- The Environment Act of 2000 is supplemented regulations, the Conservation Areas Act and the Forestry Act. These are the main legal frameworks that support the implementation of REDD+ activities in the country.
- The National REDD+ Strategy was developed with the support of the World Bank (2013), which includes the development of a national strategy for REDD+, the establishment of a national REDD+ coordination unit, and the preparation of a national REDD+ roadmap.

In addition, the "REDD+ Work Plan" developed through the consultation of the government and key stakeholders provides updated and new funding initiatives. This results in an essential tool for the implementation of the RBP.

Finally, it is important to emphasize that the RBP is a national program, significantly involving the use of national resources and capacities. The RBP is implemented by the Government of Indonesia through the Ministry of Environment and Forestry (KLHK) and the Local Government (LG) authorities in cooperation with other relevant stakeholders.

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (Proposal 30 of 3)

### Meeting

- **West New Britain**: 162
- **East New Britain**: 74
- **Total**: 236

We are pleased to present this proposal for the REDD+ Results-Based Payment (RBP) funding. This proposal outlines our plan to implement REDD+ activities in West and East New Britain provinces, using the funds provided by the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The proposed activities include:

- Introduction of the law on benefits
- Change existing departmental administrative arrangements
- Establishment of a provincial REDD+ task force
- Development of a provincial REDD+ strategy in decision-making processes
- Development of environmental safeguards in existing laws
- Development of a grievance and redress mechanism

### Socio-Economic Considerations

Stakeholder engagement has been a crucial part of REDD+ activities since the inception of the REDD+ process. The proposed activities will continue to engage stakeholders, including local communities, provincial governments, and non-governmental organizations (NGOs). This engagement is essential for the successful implementation of key components of REDD+, such as:

- **REDD+ Strategy**
- **Monitoring, Reporting, and Verification (MRV)**
- **Benefit Sharing**

These considerations involve a wide range of stakeholders including community leaders and representatives from various sectors. The proposed activities will be implemented in accordance with the principles and guidelines established by the Climate, Community, and Biodiversity Standards (CCBS) and the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC).

The proposed activities are designed to:

- **Support REDD+ implementation**: e.g., PMU as the entity responsible for monitoring forest management and forest health indicators.
- **Promote sustainable development**: e.g., the Department of National Planning and Monitoring, as the agency responsible for overall coordination with other government agencies and line ministries.
- **Enhance community participation**: e.g., through the establishment of provincial REDD+ task forces that will be implemented by REDD+ provinces, such as the province and local communities.

These activities are designed to promote the sustainable use of natural resources and improve the livelihoods of local communities. The proposed activities will also contribute to the achievement of national development goals and international commitments.

### Executive Summary

The REDD+ Funding Proposal is designed to support the implementation of the National Engagement Strategy (NES) for the period 2013-2016. The proposal aims to enhance the capacity of the Government of Indonesia to effectively implement its REDD+ activities, including the development and implementation of a Monitoring, Reporting, and Verification (MRV) system.

### Introduction

The Government of Indonesia has been actively engaged in the preparation and implementation of its REDD+ strategy since 2009. The NES was developed as part of the Government's commitment to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) and the Copenhagen Accord. The NES outlines the Government's plans for reducing greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, and for enhancing carbon stocks in Indonesia.

### Objectives and Outcomes

The overall objective of the REDD+ Funding Proposal is to support the implementation of the NES by providing financial resources for technical assistance, capacity building, and MRV activities. The specific outcomes that will be achieved through the implementation of the proposal include:

- Enhanced capacity of the Government of Indonesia to effectively implement its REDD+ strategy.
- Development and implementation of a comprehensive MRV system.
- Increased awareness and understanding of REDD+ among stakeholders in Indonesia.

### Activities

#### Activity 1: National Engagement Strategy

The first activity involves the development and implementation of the NES. This includes the preparation of a detailed work plan, the identification of key stakeholders, and the establishment of partnerships with international organizations and other stakeholders.

#### Activity 2: Technical Assistance for HFCs

The second activity focuses on providing technical assistance to the Government of Indonesia in the development and implementation of its Hydrofluorocarbon (HFC) management strategy. This includes the provision of expertise and resources to support the Government's efforts to reduce emissions from HFCs.

#### Activity 3: Capacity Building

The third activity involves the development and implementation of a capacity building program for stakeholders in Indonesia. This includes the provision of training, workshops, and other activities designed to enhance the skills and knowledge of stakeholders in REDD+ and related issues.

#### Activity 4: Monitoring, Reporting, and Verification (MRV)

The fourth activity focuses on the development and implementation of an MRV system for the Government of Indonesia. This includes the provision of technical assistance, equipment, and other resources to support the Government's efforts to monitor, report, and verify its greenhouse gas emissions.

#### Activity 5: Coordination and Management

The fifth activity involves the coordination and management of the REDD+ Funding Proposal. This includes the development of a detailed work plan, the establishment of partnerships with international organizations and other stakeholders, and the provision of technical assistance and other resources to support the implementation of the proposal.

### Customer Requirements and Commitments and the Determination of the REDD+ Qualifications

Customer requirements and commitments have shown that there is a need for continued engagement in the NES. The Government of Indonesia has committed to the development and implementation of a comprehensive MRV system, as well as the provision of technical assistance and capacity building activities.

An example is the development of the National Guidelines on NES, which was launched in 2013. These guidelines provide detailed information on the requirements for the implementation of the NES, including the identification of key stakeholders, the establishment of partnerships with international organizations, and the provision of technical assistance and capacity building activities.

The Government of Indonesia has also committed to the development and implementation of a comprehensive MRV system. This includes the provision of technical assistance, equipment, and other resources to support the Government's efforts to monitor, report, and verify its greenhouse gas emissions.

The Government of Indonesia has also committed to the development and implementation of a capacity building program for stakeholders in Indonesia. This includes the provision of training, workshops, and other activities designed to enhance the skills and knowledge of stakeholders in REDD+ and related issues.

## Senior Technical FPIC Project input to PNG

As a result of this process, PNG published the National REDD+ FPIC Guidelines in December 2014. The guidelines were developed through extensive consultations with key stakeholders and are consistent with the requirements of the GCF.

### Use of Free Prior Informed Consent (FPIC) Benefit Sharing

The use of FPIC benefits that accrued from this project has been recognized by local communities, landowners and government officials due to the existing system of compensation. Local payments were made to landowners for their participation in the consultation process. The project also provided training on FPIC and benefit sharing to local communities and provincial governments. This was an integral part of the project implementation.

The Free Prior Informed Consent for Benefit Sharing System (REDD) implemented these outcomes and negotiated with local communities, landowners and provincial governments. These benefits were agreed upon and developed, and the project has been recognized by the government as a model for future projects. The project also provided training on FPIC and benefit sharing through national REDD+ workshops. This directly led to the development of the National REDD+ Benefit Sharing Guidelines.

# REDD+ RBP Funding Proposal

The purpose will be to comprehensively evaluate opportunities offered by the RBP to further a comprehensive and integrated REDD+ process. This cycle focuses on the essential interconnections of the three REDD+ pillars.

PRO will aim to access proceeds from RBP programs in a continuous cycle by:

- Fulfilling and enhancing social benefits domains such as FREL/FRL, NMS, and SIS in accordance with the RBP guidelines.
- Adapting the implementation of the national REDD+ strategy, ensuring its relevance and impact.
- Enhancing the capacity to implement the RBP process.

Through this proactive cycle, PRO will continuously improve its carbon accounting and safeguard measures and ensure that the monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV) system is robust and reliable.

![Figure 2: Outline of the REDD+ finance cycle](image_url)

The project activities will address the main domains of development and forest degradation in PRO, aiming to enhance the capacity of local communities to manage their natural resources. This proposal aims at the RBP, the country's national REDD and the National SFM.

# REDD+ RBP Funding Proposal

## PIF National REDD+ Strategy Vision

"To catalyse transformation (change) within the forest and land use sector towards a new responsible and sustainable future for people and nature through the effective conservation of biodiversity and ecosystem services alongside ensuring that Redd+ New Outcomes are achieved."

### National Drivers for Responsible Sustainable Development (PIF Drivers) include for:

- "Climate change impacts, including aspects on water and food security, along with associated risks and vulnerabilities and generated fear or risk to people's livelihoods, with the aim of contributing to climate resilience."
- "Deforestation and forest degradation in PNG."

### Deforestation and Forest Degradation in PNG

Deforestation and forest degradation have been ongoing since the 1970s. The main drivers include logging, agriculture, mining, infrastructure development, population growth, and poor governance. These activities have contributed to significant deforestation and forest degradation.

The impact of commercial agriculture has significantly increased since 2000, due to the large expansion of oil palm plantations in the last decade. Logging activities by both foreign and national companies have also been a major contributor to deforestation and forest degradation.

These trends are closely tied to ongoing population growth, which has an annual growth rate of 3.1% and is likely to continue. This growth is driven by increased life expectancy and improved health services. The impact of these drivers on deforestation and forest degradation is significant.

Between 2000 and 2015, deforestation affected approximately 7 million hectares, leading to severe impacts on biodiversity and ecosystem integrity.

The main causes of deforestation include logging, agriculture, mining, infrastructure development, population growth, and poor governance. Deforestation has led to significant environmental degradation.

Deforestation has also had a negative impact on the livelihoods of local communities, who rely heavily on forest resources for their subsistence. The pace at which these areas are being cleared is alarming, with an estimated 300,000 hectares lost between 2002 and 2015.

Deforestation has also had a negative impact on the livelihoods of local communities, who rely heavily on forest resources for their subsistence. The pace at which these areas are being cleared is alarming, with an estimated 300,000 hectares lost between 2002 and 2015.

Deforestation has also had a negative impact on the livelihoods of local communities, who rely heavily on forest resources for their subsistence. The pace at which these areas are being cleared is alarming, with an estimated 300,000 hectares lost between 2002 and 2015.

## Green Climate Fund (GCF)

### Page 37 of 9

![Image](image_url)

**Figure 4: Drivers of deforestation and forest degradation types in PNB, Source: PNB November REDD+ Strategy**

The strategy identifies the following drivers of deforestation and forest degradation:

- **Agriculture**: Expansion of large-scale industrial agriculture for export commodities such as palm oil, rubber, pulp and paper.
  - A lack of consultation to implement best land use planning. PNB has significant challenges in aligning local and national policies with international demand.
  - Fragmented Policies and Regulatory Framework: The forestry sector operates under laws and regulations that are not aligned with the principles of sustainable development. This includes overlapping jurisdictions between the Ministry of Environment and Forestry (KLHK) and the Ministry of Agriculture.

- **Lack of technical training for local technical personnel**: Provincial and district-level forest officers lack the capacity to implement REDD+ activities.
  - Weak enforcement and implementation capacity of forest authorities: The enforcement challenges are exacerbated by a lack of resources, including equipment and vehicles. This is compounded by a lack of understanding of the legal framework governing forest management.

- **Lack of land tenure security**: Land tenure insecurity is a major issue in Indonesia, where the Forestry Act 1999 excludes customary and community land from protection under the law.
  - Lack of access to credit: Farmers and smallholders often do not have access to formal financial services. This limits their ability to invest in sustainable agricultural practices.

# REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (Page 3 of 9)

### Summary

- Promotes sustainable low-carbon development.
- Supports and conserves biodiversity.

### Background

Recent developments of a few economy and well-established institutions have led to the emergence of new technologies and knowledge. Many villages, towns, and cities are now implementing renewable energy solutions and reducing their carbon footprint. This has led to a significant increase in the number of clean energy projects worldwide.

- **Sustainable FPIC Process**: Ensures benefits are achieved through inclusive and equitable ETC as well as joint management.
- **Enhanced MRV**: Improves monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV) systems for enhanced transparency and accountability.
- **Increased Co-Benefits**: Enhances co-benefits such as improved livelihoods, health, and resilience to climate change.

### Project Objectives

The project aims to achieve the following objectives:

1. **Reduce Emissions**: Reduce greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.
2. **Enhance Livelihoods**: Enhance the livelihoods of local communities through sustainable land management practices.
3. **Conserve Biodiversity**: Conserve biodiversity by protecting forests and other ecosystems.

### Assumptions

1. The project will be implemented in countries with significant deforestation rates.
2. The project will be supported by national governments and international organizations.
3. There is a strong commitment to reducing emissions and conserving biodiversity.
4. Strong support will be provided during the implementation phase by both National and Provincial governments.

### CCDA

The CCDA supports the Forest Carbon Partnership Facility (FCPF) and UN-REDD programs. Insured significant efforts are being made to ensure that REDD+ projects are implemented in a manner consistent with national policies, strategies, and priorities.

- To key output will be to consistently deliver, along with targeted strategies to meet the specific needs of the project.

- Capacity building in governance and community development will be achieved through training and empowerment of local stakeholders and communities.
- The project will contribute to the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity and ecosystem services.
- The project will contribute to the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, and the enhancement of carbon stocks.
- The project will contribute to the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity and ecosystem services.
- The project will contribute to the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity and ecosystem services.

## Project Structure

The project will be implemented at the Forest Carbon Unit (FCU) level, including 3 main phases. The implementation of the components is based on the following sequence:

1. Component 1: Preparation phase - includes the development of a detailed project design document.
2. Component 2: Implementation phase - includes the execution of activities and monitoring.
3. Component 3: Closure phase - includes the final evaluation, reporting, and closure.

The implementation will be carried out in a mutually supportive fashion. Technical cooperation among relevant areas is essential for achieving the project objective. Additionally, the project will ensure that local and indigenous participation is promoted and supported throughout the project cycle.

The project will be implemented by the Forest Carbon Unit (FCU) of the Ministry of Environment, with technical assistance from the World Bank. The project will be financed through a combination of grants and loans.

A partial set of the phases is outlined below, indicating the Gender Action Plan (GAP) EU to ensure gender equality in the implementation process:

- Phase 1: Preparation
- Phase 2: Implementation
- Phase 3: Closure

Monitoring of the GCF-B project will be carried out through regular monitoring and evaluation activities. The project will also include a gender action plan to ensure that gender equality is promoted throughout the project cycle.

# REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (Page 4 of 9)

Training sessions will be conducted for project staff and local stakeholders to build capacity in general principles of environmental management.

Simultaneously, the project will develop and implement the Environmental and Social Management Plan (ESMP), which will include:

- Environmental impact assessments
- Gender action plans
- Indigenous peoples' policies
- Stakeholder engagement strategies

These documents will be developed through a participatory approach involving local communities, indigenous peoples, and other stakeholders. The ESMP will also include detailed procedures to guide the implementation of these policies.

The project will be implemented in close collaboration with the Government of Indonesia (GoI) and other relevant national authorities. The project will be designed to promote gender equality and empower women as active participants in all phases of the project cycle, including planning, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation.

Engaging communities effectively is fundamental to project success, which will be facilitated through engagement with local stakeholders, including indigenous peoples, community groups, and other relevant actors. The project will also ensure that these groups are actively involved throughout the project lifecycle.

To ensure a clear mapping of the existing policies, the Theory of Change (ToC) will be updated. This general theory will be developed in close collaboration with key stakeholders to identify gaps and opportunities for improvement. The ToC will be used as an ongoing reference point to guide implementation and monitoring activities.

A monitoring framework will be established to track progress against project objectives. This framework will include indicators that are relevant to the project's goals, including gender equality, environmental management, and social inclusion. The framework will also ensure that data is collected in a timely manner and used effectively for decision-making.

By way of this Invitation Phase, the project will use a fully developed Gender Action Plan, as approved by the GCF Board, which includes specific actions to promote gender equality and empower women. The project will also develop a Theory of Change (ToC) and a Monitoring Framework within the first six months of implementation. These foundational documents are essential for ensuring that the project is on track to achieve its objectives.

### Activities

- **Activity 1:** Update and implement the Project Gender Action Plan.
- **Activity 2:** Develop and implement the Environmental and Social Management Plan, and engagement with local stakeholders.
- **Activity 3:** Update and implement the Theory of Change (ToC).
- **Activity 4:** Develop a Monitoring Framework.
- **Activity 5:** Implement a gender-responsive approach to measurement and evaluation of national and provincial governments' policies and programs.

### Context

The project will be implemented in close collaboration with relevant government agencies, including the main national technical bodies that lead use planning, management, and monitoring activities. The project will also engage local communities and other stakeholders to ensure effective implementation.

The activities will be conducted in accordance with the REDD+ A Framework of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) and the decisions of the Conference of the Parties to the UNFCCC. # REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (Proposal #4 of 9)

### Abstract

Aim: To reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance carbon stocks in tropical forests by promoting sustainable land use, reducing deforestation, and improving forest management.

Objectives:
- Reduce emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.
- Enhance forest carbon stocks.
- Improve the livelihoods of local communities through sustainable forest management.

### Current Situation

Greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation are a major threat to climate change mitigation. The proposal aims to reduce these emissions by promoting sustainable land use, reducing deforestation, and enhancing forest carbon stocks in tropical forests.

### Activities to be carried out under the activity include:

- **Activity 1: Identification of family-based businesses**
  - Identify and document family-based businesses.
  - Provide training on sustainable business practices.

- **Activity 2: Capacity building for local communities**
  - Conduct training sessions on sustainable forest management.
  - Provide technical assistance to local communities.

### Activities will be also linked to Output 2.2, providing capacity building support to the development of water-related activities and sustainable land use:

- **Output 2.1: Enhance sustainable livelihoods and improve food security**
  - Develop and implement sustainable agriculture practices.
  - Promote agroforestry systems.

### Based on the PRA results in Activity Area "Permanent economic productivity and sustainable livelihoods":

Over 80% of the RBP population is rural and relies on family-based agricultural activities for both subsistence and income. The majority of these families have been farming for generations, with most having farmed for over 20 years.

### Implementation Strategy

The project will be implemented through a participatory approach involving local communities, government agencies, and non-governmental organizations (NGOs). The strategy includes:

- **Community Engagement**
  - Conduct community meetings to discuss the project.
  - Establish community-based monitoring systems.

This proposal outlines a comprehensive approach to reducing greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation. Management of existing Red Sphere projects and other activities to ensure continued financial sustainability and long-term viability.

The project will promote sustainable financial mechanisms relevant to PNG's local context, enabling smallholder farmers to access credit and agricultural inputs. The proposed funding will cover the costs associated with implementing the project, including training, capacity building, and monitoring and evaluation. This will enable the project to achieve its objectives of improving food security, enhancing rural livelihoods, and increasing incomes for smallholder farmers.

Efforts will include sustainable land management practices and technologies to increase carbon sequestration, such as agroforestry, conservation agriculture, and integrated pest management. These activities will also contribute to improved soil health, biodiversity, and ecosystem services.

These measures aim to strengthen long-term food security while boosting long-term socio-economic development and environmental sustainability. The project will promote sustainable land use practices that enhance agricultural productivity, improve livelihoods, and reduce greenhouse gas emissions. It will also contribute to the achievement of national climate change mitigation targets.

3. **Carbon Sequestration:**

### Objectives

- To support the conservation and sustainable use of forests.
- To reduce greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.
- To promote the development of low-emission, climate-resilient development pathways.

### Methodology

The project will be implemented through a combination of direct investments in forest conservation activities and payments for ecosystem services. The project will also support capacity building and knowledge sharing to enhance the effectiveness of REDD-plus activities.

# REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Selection of Targeting Provinces

The project will focus on implementation of direct actions in six targeted provinces: West Sepik, East Sepik, Madang, Morobe, Milne Bay and Gulf. These provinces were selected based on their potential to balance provincial objectives of livelihoods and ecosystem services with the need for carbon finance. The selection process was guided by the following criteria:

1. Provinces with high performance in reducing emissions.
2. Provinces valued for shared border locations between cross-border land use change and high deforestation rates.
3. Provinces that may have a higher risk of deforestation and forest degradation because of their proximity to logging concessions or mining operations.

To calculate the average ranking for province prioritization, different weights were allocated as follows:

Average Ranking = (0.5 * R1) + (0.2 * R2) + (0.3 * R3)

Where:
- **R1** is the rank taking into account provincial performance.
- **R2** is the ranking considering proximity to cross-border land use change.
- **R3** is the ranking associated with the Population Density.

The targeted provinces cover an area of 127,846 km² and are home to over 1.5 million people. The provinces were selected based on their potential for REDD+ implementation and their need for carbon finance. The provinces include:

- **West Sepik**
- **East Sepik**
- **Madang**
- **Morobe**
- **Milne Bay**
- **Gulf**

# REDD+ Hub RBP Funding Proposal

## Green Climate Fund (Project #GF)

Harness and natural discoveries towards the provision of basic needs with food accessibility, healthcare, and other social services.

West New Britain Province is located in the northeastern part of Papua New Guinea. It covers an area of approximately 20,865 km² and has a population of about 374,918 people. The province comprises six districts: Talasea, Nigo, Kandrian-Gloucester, West Talasea, East New Britain, and the Autonomous Region of Bougainville. The province is home to diverse ecosystems, including rainforests, mangroves, coral reefs, and marine life. The region has a rich cultural heritage and is known for its traditional practices.

The proposed project will focus on providing basic needs such as food security, healthcare, and other social services. It aims to improve the livelihood of the people in West New Britain Province by addressing the root causes of poverty and inequality. The project will also promote sustainable development and environmental conservation.

### Benefits

As the community is located near the plant, it will be the planted beneficiaries of the project. Plantation establishment and other activities will provide employment opportunities for the local communities. This will lead to an increase in income levels and improve living standards. The project will also contribute to food security by increasing agricultural productivity and reducing post-harvest losses.

#### Target Beneficiaries

| Beneficiary Group | Description |
|------------------|-------------|
| **Public institutions** | Government agencies such as the Department of Agriculture, Livestock, and Forestry (DALF) and the National Fisheries Authority (NFA). |
| **Private sector** | Local businesses that will benefit from increased demand for goods and services. |
| **Community leaders** | Community leaders who will be involved in project implementation and monitoring. |
| **Academia** | Academic institutions such as universities and research centers that will conduct studies on the impact of the project. |
| **Civil Society academia** | Non-governmental organizations (NGOs) and community-based organizations (CBOs) that will be involved in project implementation and advocacy. |

### Project Components

1. **Component 1**: Establishment of a food security program.
2. **Component 2**: Establishment of a healthcare program.
3. **Component 3**: Establishment of an environmental conservation program.

The project will be implemented over the next five years, with each component contributing to the overall goal of improving livelihoods and promoting sustainable development in West New Britain Province.

# REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (Page 08 of 9)

At the highest level, the FAO Roma Headquarters Office will be the GEF Accredited Entity responsible for managing and implementing the project. The project will be implemented at the national level by the National Designated Authority (NDA), in direct contact with the GCF.

At the national level, a Project Board will be established, which will include representatives from the NDA, the Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (MAEDD), the Ministry of Agriculture, Cattle, Fisheries and Forestry (MAPA), the Ministry of Planning and Budgeting (MEP), implementing the Government of Haiti, the Prime Minister's Office (PME) and the Governor of the Central Bank (BRH).

The general responsibilities of the Project Board will be providing overall representation, guidance, reviews and approvals. The Project Board will also provide strategic direction to the project implementation process. The project team and/or main agency that can call upon the Project Board for advice and support.

Project Board will be the government committee and comprises with the National REDD+ Committee, including the Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (MAEDD), the Ministry of Planning and Budgeting (MEP), the Prime Minister's Office (PME) and the Governor of the Central Bank (BRH).

The project team will be composed of the following agencies: the NDA, the MAEDD, the MEP, the PME and the BRH. The Project Team is a multi-agency group that will work together to implement the project.

During the implementation phase, the Project Board will be supported by the Project Implementation Unit (PIU) funded by the GCF. The PIU's main tasks are to provide technical assistance to the Project Board and to ensure that the project is implemented in accordance with the approved project document. The PIU will also be responsible for monitoring the progress of the project, providing technical support to the Project Board and ensuring that the project is implemented in accordance with the approved project document.

The National REDD+ Committee will be composed of representatives from the MAEDD, the MEP, the PME and the BRH. The National REDD+ Committee will be responsible for providing strategic direction to the project implementation process and for ensuring that the project is implemented in accordance with the approved project document.

The project team will be composed of representatives from the NDA, the MAEDD, the MEP, the PME and the BRH. The project team will be responsible for implementing the project and for ensuring that it is implemented in accordance with the approved project document.

At the Project Level for each of the five implemented provinces, the PIU will coordinate the project implementation process through a local working group composed of representatives from the provincial government, civil society organizations and other stakeholders. The local working groups will be responsible for implementing the project at the provincial level and for ensuring that it is implemented in accordance with the approved project document.

At this level, the activities are in close proximity (technical and practical institutional coordination) between the central government and the provinces. This will ensure the sustainability of results and the effective implementation of activities and interventions engaged in the project areas involved.

# REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (Project ID: 27)

- Supports initiatives that address climate change impacts in biodiversity and watersheds.
- Works of REDD+ programs by managing forest conservation areas according to the principles of sustainable development.
- Promote training and support to communities, government agencies, and other stakeholders.
- Maintain and improve the management of protected areas, including the implementation of the National Biodiversity Strategy Action Plan (NBSAP), the Physical Framework Act, UNDP A. Redd+ activities, and other relevant laws.
- Maintain and update land-use planning, including agriculture, forestry, mining, and tourism.
- Adaptation to climate change, disaster risk reduction, and management of water resources for sustainable development.
- Promote the use of renewable energy sources such as solar, wind, hydro, and biomass.
- Develop and implement national policies related to land use, environment, and climate change.
- Reduce greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.
- Support community-based natural resource management and biodiversity conservation.
- Contribute to the achievement of the Millennium Development Goals (MDGs) and other relevant international agreements.

### Objectives

- Strengthen national capacities for sustainable development and climate change adaptation.
- Promote the use of renewable energy sources such as solar, wind, hydro, and biomass.
- Develop and implement national policies related to land use, environment, and climate change.
- Reduce greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.
- Support community-based natural resource management and biodiversity conservation.

### Activities

- Training and implementation of climate change policies, strategies, and programs.
- Development of strategic plans which align with the NBSAP and REDD+ activities.
- Promotion of sustainable development in protected areas and other ecosystems.
- Support for local organizations and communities to implement sustainable practices.
- Support initiatives that address climate change impacts in biodiversity and watersheds.

### Results

- Strengthen national capacities for sustainable development and climate change adaptation.
- Promote the use of renewable energy sources such as solar, wind, hydro, and biomass.
- Develop and implement national policies related to land use, environment, and climate change.
- Reduce greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.
- Support community-based natural resource management and biodiversity conservation.

### Monitoring and Evaluation

- Monitor the implementation of REDD+ activities in alignment with national policies and international agreements.
- Monitor the impact of REDD+ activities on local communities, including their participation and benefits.
- Monitor the effectiveness of climate change adaptation measures and their impact on sustainable development.

### Collaboration

- A broad coalition and coordination on issues management between a research and academic institution.
- Collaboration with the Physical Framework Commission on regulation and enforcement of environmental laws.
- Collaboration with national and international organizations to promote sustainable development.
- Collaboration with local governments, communities, and other stakeholders in the implementation of REDD+ activities.

### Funding

- The funding will be used for the implementation of REDD+ activities, including training, capacity building, and monitoring and evaluation of climate change adaptation measures.

### Page 7: Implementation arrangements scheme

PNG's forests are among the highest priority destinations in the world. They are home to over 10,000 species of plants and animals, including 260 bird species, more than 50% of which are endemic. PNG's forests also provide vital ecosystem services such as carbon sequestration, water purification, soil conservation, and habitat for a wide range of biodiversity. The government has committed to reducing emissions from deforestation and forest degradation (REDD+) by 2030.

These funds (if a normal year is the model) would ensure the survival, and economic viability, of communities that depend on forests. The funds will also support the development of sustainable livelihoods for local communities, including those in remote areas. This will help to reduce poverty and improve the quality of life for people living in these areas.

The funds will be used to support the implementation of REDD+ activities, such as:

- **Capacity building**: Providing training and technical assistance to local communities and government agencies.
- **Community engagement**: Supporting community-based monitoring and verification systems.
- **Law enforcement**: Strengthening law enforcement and legal frameworks for forest conservation.
- **Sustainable development**: Promoting sustainable development initiatives that benefit local communities.

Additionally, the funds will support the development of sustainable livelihoods for local communities, including the establishment of sustainable enterprises. This will help to reduce poverty and improve the quality of life for people living in these areas.

Furthermore, REDD+ activities will contribute to the achievement of national climate change targets and other international commitments.

# REDD+ HFC RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (Proj. ID #)

The ultimate goal and key objective of this funding proposal is to demonstrate green development in only two provinces in Thailand: Chiang Mai and Mae Hong Son. These provinces are located near the border with Myanmar, where deforestation, illegal logging and forest encroachment have been ongoing for decades. The project will be implemented by the Department of National Parks, Wildlife and Plant Conservation (DNP). Many of these resources are crucial for the livelihood security of local communities.

At the highest level, the expected carbon emission reductions benefits of the implementation of the NFS team would be:

- 5.2 million hectares of forest under conservation
- 4.8 thousand square kilometers for which habitat corridor is secured
- $17 million in annual revenue from carbon credits
- $30 million in annual revenue from other sources

For additional information, please refer to the full proposal document.

### COMPONENT 1: Increased institutional capacity and natural and social environment in Thailand

- **Enhanced Tenure and Land Use Security**: Integrated management and harmonization of security rights for customary land tenure and community forestry. Including the ability to secure FPIC (free, prior and informed consent).
- **Increased Inclusivity in Decision Making**: Greater participation of women, youth and disadvantaged groups in decision making processes.
- **Enhanced Capacity**: Enhanced capacity by providing appropriate training and technical support for local communities.
- **Policy Dialogue and Knowledge Sharing**: A comprehensive knowledge base and network to inform evidence-based policy dialogue.

### COMPONENT 2: Enhanced economic productivity and sustainable livelihoods of customary land users

- **Climate Change Adaptation Impact**: Strengthening institutional capacity to address climate change adaptation impacts on customary land use.
- **Biodiversity Protection**: Integrated sub-national management of FPIC, biodiversity and ecosystem services over 400,000 hectares.
- **Sustainable Forest Management**: Sustainable forest management practices such as agro-forestry, community-based forest management and sustainable harvesting.
- **Conservation Linked Tourism Opportunities**: Developing new tourism opportunities associated with conservation linked to local communities.
- **Support Food Security**: Improved agricultural productivity and diversification, increasing food security for local communities.

## Business Agricultural Revenue - Higher revenue for sustainable agricultural production, supported by improved practices, market access, and more productive value chains.

### R & D Methods of Measurement

PND has demonstrated one of the highest-growing economies globally in the 21st century, with an average annual growth rate of over 6%. This growth has been driven by foreign investment in the natural gas sector and other extractive industries. However, this growth has also led to significant environmental degradation.

Despite these gains, PND has faced fiscal challenges. By 2019, spending on public services had increased by 17% since 2016, while revenue from oil and gas had fallen by 84%. This has led to a budget deficit of $3 billion in 2019.

Governments are increasingly focusing on green growth as a means to achieve sustainable development goals. The government is committed to achieving the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) by 2030, including SDG 7 (affordable and clean energy), SDG 8 (decent work and economic growth), and SDG 15 (life on land).

The proposed project aims to empower local communities to achieve REDD+ results and contribute to the goals of the Paris Agreement. It will also help to meet the objectives of the UN Sustainable Development Goals for 2030.

Modelling additional financing to enhance REDD+ results has the same challenges as addressing investment in other sectors, such as agriculture, forestry, fisheries, and tourism. These challenges include:

- Ensuring that investments are environmentally sustainable.
- Addressing social and cultural issues related to resource use.
- Mapping potential co-benefits and synergies with other development efforts.

- High levels of investment: PHC is considered a high priority for investment, ranking 5/10 out of 10 in the Green Book. Local Development Bank, Commercial banks, and other financial institutions have shown interest in investing in PHC.
- Lack of access to finance: Despite the high demand for PHC, there are limited financing options available. Microfinance institutions provide small loans but lack the capacity to invest in large-scale projects.
- High costs of doing business: Due to its remoteness, PHC faces challenges with isolated transport infrastructure and specialized equipment and skills required for project implementation. This increases the cost of doing business significantly compared to other regions.

### REDD+ Contribution and management

The establishment of a effective REDD+ coordination and management structure will enhance PHC's effective overall for the benefit of all stakeholders and engage different groups and organizations involved in the REDD+ process. This will ensure that the REDD+ process is transparent, inclusive and accountable.

#### Social and environmental safeguards

Social and environmental safeguards are critical to the success of the NFI. Over 85% of the forests 7.9 million people live in rural areas relying on benefits from forest resources. These communities will benefit directly from the REDD+ process.

Community engagement is a key component of PHC's social and cultural activities. It accomplishes several objectives including:

- Ensuring that local communities are involved in the planning, implementation and monitoring of REDD+ activities.
- Ensuring that local communities have a voice in decision-making processes related to their forests and livelihoods.
- Ensuring that local communities benefit from the REDD+ process.

Additional information regarding the NFI is available on the PHC website. The CCDA, as a newly established agency, has been actively engaged with the REDD+ process since its inception. The CCDA has been working closely with the PHC to ensure that the safeguards of the RBP are in place and that the benefits of the RBP are shared equitably.

#### Host country coordination

Host country coordination is critical for effective implementation of the NFI. The CCDA and various sector agencies will work together to ensure that the REDD+ process is implemented effectively. This includes the approval of safeguards, the development of a monitoring system, and the establishment of a grievance mechanism.

This proposal outlines specific activities, NFIs, and relevant outcomes for the implementation of the NFI. It also highlights the importance of community engagement in the REDD+ process. Together, we can drive meaningful change that benefits our environment and communities.

### D.S. Coordination Unit

PHC has established itself as a global leader in the REDD initiative, demonstrating strong leadership and commitment to the development of effective safeguards for REDD+. The PHC has worked closely with numerous countries and organizations at both national and international levels. The NFI is designed to support these efforts by providing a framework for the implementation of REDD+ activities.

The NFI is a vital framework that has social components. The first component includes an action plan for the development of safeguards. The second component focuses on the development of a monitoring system. The third component focuses on the development of a grievance mechanism. These components are designed to ensure that the REDD+ process is transparent, inclusive and accountable.

   - The project aims to promote sustainable forest management practices with higher population density.

   - The project will support the development of national strategies for reducing emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.

### E. Compliance with GCF policies

#### E.1 Environmental and Social Safeguards

The safeguard policies are the cornerstone of the GCF's Environmental and Social Safeguards (ESA) policy, which was adopted in 2013. These policies ensure that REDD+ activities comply with the best-practiced activities and are implemented in a manner consistent with ESA as well as the GCF's social and environmental safeguard policies.

As noted in the PIF's Final Report to the FGP for the period 2015-2016, with UNDP's support, the Natural REDD+ Benefit Sharing Plan was developed. The plan was designed to ensure that the benefits from the REDD+ activities are shared fairly and equitably among the local communities.

In February 2017, the revised safeguards were adopted by the GCF Board. This revision included a new requirement for the GCF to provide information on how safeguard policies have been addressed and managed in the project's Environmental and Social Safeguards Management System (ESMS).

Notably, when the REDD+ activities are implemented, they must be consistent with the best practices of the ESA policy. The safeguards ensure that the environmental and social impacts of the project are properly managed.

As outlined in the Final Report, during the initial period, the PIF adopted the REDD+ Benefit Sharing Plan to perform implementation and establish mechanisms required by the GCF's safeguard policies. This plan was designed to address the needs of local communities and ensure that they benefit from the project.

The country's REDD+ activities were built on a wide range of technical and institutional assessments, stakeholder participation, and social and environmental impact assessments. Additionally, the ESA concluded that funding the Belem Declaration (2014-2016) has been effective in promoting sustainable development.

The funding policy and legal framework together with existing and new policies aimed to protect rights and promote sustainable development are aligned with the objectives of FAO and relevant national laws. Notably, PNG's legal and governance framework also supports the REDD+ process and the need for a multi-sectoral approach to address underlying causes of deforestation and forest degradation.

As required by decision 1/CP.2, over the REDD+ Readiness period, joint efforts between the Government and civil society have been made to develop a national strategy on the management of all natural resources, including forests. This has been developed in consultation with relevant stakeholders, including community-based organizations and communities.

However, it is worth noting that, given the high level of dependence on REDD+ financing in PNG, there is the need for a comprehensive approach to address the underlying causes of deforestation and forest degradation. This includes addressing the drivers of deforestation and forest degradation, such as illegal logging, land use change, and unsustainable agricultural practices.

# REDD+ Hub RBP Funding Proposal

Green Climate Fund (Project ID: 70)

## Executive Summary

This proposal outlines the activities and deliverables for the REDD+ Hub's Request for Proposals (RFP) funding under the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The project aims to support countries in their efforts to reduce emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, conservation of forests, sustainable management of forests, enhancement of forest carbon stocks, and the enhancement of biodiversity.

### Objectives

1. **Screening, Documentation, Assessment**: Conduct comprehensive screening, documentation, assessment, and reporting of environmental, social, governance, human rights, and operational risks.
2. **Standardization**: Develop standardized procedures to guide countries in preparing, submitting, and implementing proposals under the REDD+ framework.
3. **Capacity Building**: Provide capacity-building support to countries to enhance their knowledge on safeguards and implementation of the terms and conditions of the GCF.
4. **Coordination**: Facilitate coordination among stakeholders, including governments, civil society organizations, and private sector entities.

### Funding Requirements

The total funding required for this proposal is USD 10 million over a period of five years. This includes costs associated with project implementation, capacity-building activities, and administrative expenses.

## RBP Process

### Breakdown of Activities During the RFP Process

As documented in section C.3, between 2009 and 2018, the GCF conducted a series of activities to build institutions, capacities, and systems for countries to access funding under the GCF. The REDD+ Hub was established as part of these efforts.

- **Outreach and Engagement**: Conduct outreach and engagement with stakeholders.
- **Capacity Building**: Provide capacity-building support to countries.
- **Funding Mechanism**: Establish a funding mechanism for RBP projects.

### Timeline

- **October 2019**: The National REDD+ Hub was established in collaboration with the GCF.
- **November 2019**: The Hub began its operations, including outreach and capacity-building activities.
- **February 2020**: The Hub launched a call for proposals to support countries in their efforts to access funding under the GCF.

### Key Milestones

- **March 2020**: Over 50 applications were received from various countries.
- **April 2020**: Initial screening of applications was completed, and shortlisted candidates were invited for further discussions.
- **May 2020**: Detailed assessments of the shortlisted proposals were conducted.

### Sustainability

The REDD+ Hub aims to support long-term sustainable development by promoting conservation and sustainable use of forests. The project will also contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), particularly SDG 15, which focuses on life on land.

## Conclusion

This proposal outlines the activities and deliverables for the REDD+ Hub's RFP funding under the GCF. The project aims to support countries in their efforts to reduce emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, conservation of forests, sustainable management of forests, enhancement of forest carbon stocks, and the enhancement of biodiversity.
